Let's design optimization algorithm in code:
- Since homogeneous, for a candidate length n, normalize sequence to sum=1 to compute objective C=2n max(conv). But evaluate_sequence with sum small returns inf if sum<0.01! Important: evaluate_sequence checks sum_a <0.01 return inf. So final sequence must have sum ≥0.01. In search, we can use normalized sum=1 to compute objective, then scale final to e.g. sum=10 (safe). Objective invariant to scaling (as long >0.01). For evaluate_sequence calls, we can return scaled sequence with sum maybe 1 to avoid inf (1 >0.01). Good.
- All values nonnegative; can be up to 1000 but we keep small.

Objective for direct sequence x length n:
score = 2n max(conv(x,x)) / sum(x)^2. To compare, use sum=1: cost = 2n max(conv(x/xsum, x/xsum)) = 2n max(conv(x,x))/sum^2.

Implementing LP with scipy:
```
from scipy.optimize import linprog
def lp_direction(f, M, gmax=100.0):
    n=len(f)
    # Build A_ub as dense? constraints 2n-1 x n.
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
    # A[k, j] = f[?] such that sum_i A[k,j] g_j = (f*g)[k]
    # (f*g)[k] = sum_{i} f_i g_{k-i}, with j=k-i => i=k-j. So A[k,j] = f[k-j] if 0<=k-j<n else 0.
    for k in range(2*n-1):
        js = max(0, k-(n-1)) to min(n-1,k)
        A[k, js] = f[ks] where ks = k-js
    c = -np.ones(n)
    b = np.full(2*n-1, M * (1+1e-9))
    bounds = [(0,gmax)]*n
    res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    return res.x if res.success else f
```
Dense A size (2n-1)*n ~4n^2. For n=256, 511*256=130k floats, OK. For n=1024, 2M floats, maybe memory 16MB, okay but LP slow. We can limit n≤256 for LP. For larger, use subgradient.

But note: The convolution in LP uses f and g where g is same length; c_k = (f*g)[k] = Σ_i f_i g_{k-i}, matches linear conv. Good.

Maybe simpler: Since f*f coefficients should be flat, we can directly do coordinate-wise iterative "ratio of target to current" in time? For multiplicative? If Q=f*f, we want Q normalized. But Q is pair products. Could use EM? For a bipartite graph of pairs (i,j) with sum k, q_k = Σ_{edge in class k} v_i v_j. We seek v such that row sums (classes) equal target. This is like matrix scaling: Treat v_i v_j as weights on edges of a graph where vertices are variables and edges connect i and j if i+j in class? Actually complete graph with edge weight v_i v_j. We want sum of weights on edges for each color k equal target. This is a "factor graph" with unary factors v_i. We can solve by iterative scaling: For each k, if q_k > target, reduce variables involved; if q_k < target, increase. This is similar to the greedy algorithm for proportional fair. Algorithm:
- Initialize v positive, target q_k = 1 for all k (or current sum normalized).
- Iterate: compute q_k. For each active k with q_k > T, multiply variables i that participate in class k by factor <1; for q_k < T, multiply by >1. But variables participate in many k. To avoid disturbances, use small updates: v_i *= exp( -η Σ_k λ_k * 2 * x_{k-i} ) where λ_k = smoothed(q_k/T -1). This is exactly subgradient with log and normalization. Might find flat q.

This is our subgradient idea. With target flat q, we can minimize max by penalizing deviations above target (which increases with iterations). Good.

Key helper: `score_internal(seq)`:
```
x=np.asarray(seq,float)
x=np.clip(x,0,1000)
s=x.sum()
if s<1e-12: return inf
q=np.convolve(x,x)
return 2.0*len(x)*q.max()/ (s*s)
```

Subgradient update:
Given x (1D length n, sum= maybe 1). Compute Q=np.convolve(x,x). Choose active weights:
Option A: top `K` indices with q > tau*M. Let M=max(Q). If M==0 return.

Gradient for q_k: derivative w.r.t x_j = 2 x_{k-j} if 0≤k-j<n. We need combine over active k: G_j = 2 Σ_{k} w_k x_{k-j}. This is convolution of active weights (reversed?) with x.
Let W length 2n-1 (weights over k). We want for each j, sum_k W[k]*x[k-j] with valid. This is cross-correlation. We can compute via np.convolve(W, x) or loop? Let H[k-j] = x_{k-j}. Then x[j-i], not x[i-j]. We need x[k-j] = x[-(j-k)]. This is correlation. `np.correlate(W, x, mode='full')`? Numpy correlate(a,v) returns Σ a[n+k] conj(v[k])? Let's avoid. def optimize_sequence(x, time_limit, method='lp+random'):
   n=len(x)
   best=x; bestcost=cost_norm(x)
   start=bestcopy
   while time<limit:

      # if stuck, mutate more aggressively or restart from initial
```

def improve_sequence(x0, budget_s, seed):
  n=len(x0)
  x=copy; best=x; bestcost=cost_normal(x)
  failed=0
  t=0.5; step=0.1
  while time<...:
     # Choose operation randomly:
     op = rng.random()
     if n<=256 and op<0.2: candidate=lp_direction(x)
     elif op<0.6: candidate=subgradient_step(x, top_k, step)
     elif op<0.85: candidate=block_perturb(x, rng, sigma)
     else: candidate=coordinate_perturb(x)
     x = renorm(clip(candidate)); cost=cost_normal(x)
     if cost<bestcost: best=x; bestcost=cost; failed=0; maybe reduce step
     else: failed +=1; if failed>...: reinitialize x from best with larger perturbation or from starts
```
But subgradient may not improve monotonic; use line search: Try multiple step sizes [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0] and accept best.

Subgradient step implementation:
```
def subgradient_step(x, K=20, temp=0.1, step=0.2):
   q=np.convolve(x,x)
   M=q.max()
   if M<=0: return x.copy()
   # weights: all q > 0.8M or top K
   order=np.argsort(q)[::-1]
   active=order[:min(K,len(q))]
   w=np.zeros(len(q)); w[active]=1.0/len(active)
   # optional temperature weights: for active, w proportional exp(q/temp)
   G=np.zeros(n)
   for k in active:
      start=max(0,k-(n-1)); stop=min(n-1,k)+1
      G[start:stop] += 2.0 * x[start:stop][::-1]
   # scale gradient by 1/(max(G)+eps)
   gscale=G.max()
   if gscale<=0: return x
   # log update
   y=np.log(x+1e-300) - step * G/gscale
   # ensure nonneg exp; clip lower
   xnew=np.exp(np.clip(y, -30, 30))
   xnew=np.clip(xnew,0,1000)
   s=xnew.sum()
   if s<=0: return np.zeros(n)
   return xnew/s
```
But this only decreases variables in high q; renormalization may increase all. Need step maybe 0.5. We can also include an opposing term for low q to increase them: Use weights w = softmax((q - M)/temp) with negative? Actually to reduce max, we want reduce high-contributing variables. Low q variables not touched; renorm increases them indirectly. Good.

```
def build_lp_indices(n):
  rows=[]; cols=[]
  for k in range(2n-1):
     start=max(0,k-n+1); stop=min(n-1,k)+1
     idx=np.arange(start,stop)
     rows.extend([k]*len(idx)); cols.extend(idx.tolist())
  return np.array(rows,int), np.array(cols,int)
```
Data for f: for each k, values f[start:stop][::-1]. We can fill by loop.
Could precompute for each row the slice parameters and use vectorization? Loop okay.

Let's enhance prefix initialization for length L. Coefficients: use recurrence to generate exact prefix with M=1:
a[0]=1
for k in 1..L-1:
   R = 2*sum_{i=1}^{k-1} a[i]*a[k-i]  # since a0=1, q_k=2a[k]+R if active
   a[k] = max(0, (1 - R)/2)
This is O(L^2) but for L=512 fine. This produces central binomial-ish coefficients. Scale sum not important.
Let's verify L=6: a0=1,a1=.5,a2=.375,a3=(1-2*.5*.375)/2=(1-.375)/2=.3125, a4=(1-2*.5*.3125-2*.375^2)/2=(1-.3125-.28125)/2=.203125 (not 0.273; central binomial exact 0.273? Wait recurrence q4 active: q4=2a0a4+2a1a3+2a2a2. 2a1a3=0.3125, 2a2^2=0.28125, sum0.59375, a4=0.203125. But earlier I gave 0.2734375 from binomial coefficients. Which is correct for q_k=1? Let's check binomial coefficients of (1-x)^-1/2: 1,0.5,0.375,0.3125,0.2734375. Compute q4 for these: 2*a0*a4 +2a1a3+2a2^2 =0.546875 +0.3125+0.28125=1.140625 >1. So prefix does NOT make q4=1! My recurrence from q_k=M for all k is wrong because q_k includes 2a_k a0 only if a0? Yes q4 includes 2a4? Actually q4 = a0a4 + a1a3 + a2a2 + a3a1 + a4a0 =2a4+2a1a3+a2^2 (not 2a2^2). I mistakenly doubled a2^2. Correct: if k even, middle term a_{k/2}^2 not doubled. Recurrence R includes 2a1a3 + a2^2 =0.3125+0.140625=0.453125, a4=(1-0.453125)/2=0.2734375. Good. So use formula: R=2Σ_{i=1}^{k-1, i<k-i} a_i a_{k-i} + (if k even) a_{k/2}^2. Then a_k=(1-R)/2.

We can generate prefix efficiently via convolution recurrence: q_k so far? For k from0 to L-1, before setting a[k], R=Σ_{i=1}^{k-1} a[i]a[k-i] (with a0=1) but q_k=2a[k]+R. If we maintain existing q_k from previous a's? Since a[k] not set, R is the part excluding i=0,k. We can compute loop O(L^2) fine.
```
def make_prefix(n):
 a=np.zeros(n); a[0]=1
 for k in range(1,n):
    R=2.0*np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1])? Need pairs i=1..k-1 product a[i]a[k-i]. `np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1])` length k-1. If k even, middle included? For dot over i=1..k-1 includes each pair once. q_k=2a0a_k + Σ_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i}? Wait Σ all i=0..k: 2a0a_k + Σ_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i} + (if? For i=k, a_k a0 included in 2. For i=0. For i in 1..k-1, each unordered pair? If i and k-i both in 1..k-1, dot includes both i and k-i separately if we sum all i=1..k-1. That's correct because convolution sum over ordered i? For q_k =Σ_{i=0}^k a_i a_{k-i}. It includes i and k-i as separate unless same. For i=1..k-1, sum includes both. Thus R=Σ_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i} (dot a[1:k] with reversed a[1:k]? example k=4: a[1:4]=[a1,a2,a3], reversed for a[k-i]? Need a[3],a[2],a[1] -> dot =a1a3+a2a2+a3a1 =2a1a3+a2^2. Correct. So R = np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1])? For k=4, a[1:4]=a1,a2,a3; a[k-1:0:-1] = a[3:0:-1]=a3,a2,a1. dot=2a1a3+a2^2. Good. Then a[k]=(1-R)/2. Clamp nonnegative.
```
This O(L^2) for 1024 ~1M, fine.

Score of prefix: compute internal. For L=6121, O(37M) to generate dot loops maybe okay ~0.05s? Python loop 6121 with dot of average 3000 => ~18M ops in C per dot? Actually each dot C loops, total ~18M multiply, okay maybe 0.2s.

```

Implementation details:

```

```

Time-domain spectral step:
```
def spectral_step(x, Nfft, alpha, beta):
   F=np.fft.rfft(x,Nfft)
   # target T length 2n-1 ones perhaps weighted (e.g., current q normalized)
   T=np.ones(2*n-1); T_hat=np.fft.rfft(T,Nfft)
   amp=np.sqrt(np.abs(T_hat)+1e-12)
   absF=np.abs(F); absF[absF<1e-12]=1e-12
   phase=F/absF
   F2=phase*amp
   y=np.fft.irfft(F2, Nfft)[:n]
   # y may have negative; take positive part and fill zeros
   y_pos=np.clip(y,0,1000)
   if sum(y_pos)>0:
       candidate=y_pos/sum(y_pos)
   else: candidate=x
   # blend with x in log domain to avoid losing positivity
   return alpha*candidate + (1-alpha)*x  # after normalize
```
This may jump; use small alpha and evaluate.

Functions:
```
import numpy as np, time, math
try:
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAS_SCIPY=True
except Exception:
    HAS_SCIPY=False

No lambda.

`normalize(x)`: clip, sum, if sum<=0 return ones, return x/sum.
`eval_cost(x)`: as above.

`make_prefix(n)`:
```
if n==1: return np.array([1.0])
a=np.zeros(n); a[0]=1.0
for k in range(1,n):
    if k==1: R=0.0
    else:
        R=float(np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1]))
    val=(1.0 - R)*0.5
    if val < 0: val=0.0
    a[k]=val
return normalize(a)  # for optimization sum1
```
But if we normalize, M changes. For LP M computed current.

`make_base_inits(n, rng)`:
- prefix = make_prefix(n)
- maybe `prefix_rev` same score but different? Reverse doesn't change cost; skip.
- `rand_exp`: rng.exponential(size=n), normalize
- `rand_gauss_abs`: abs(rng.normal)+0.1 normalize
- `two_level`: values 1 and 2 split ratio r. For contiguous: pos=int(n*r); set 1 before, 2 after. Also random permutation? Positive sequence with two values random order; could improve? We can create by random mask with target high count. Normalize. Try r in [0.3,0.4,0.5,0.6,0.7].
- `sine`: 1+a*np.sin(2π(i)/n) clipped; a in [0.3,0.6,0.9]. Also `1+a*np.cos(2πi/n)` and `1+a*np.sin(πi/(n-1))`? Ensure nonnegative.
- `power_tail`: (i+1)**0.5? maybe decreasing. `prefix` covers.
- `power_ramp`: (i+1)**p p∈[-1,-0.5,0.5].
- `spectral_prefix`: apply one spectral step to prefix with alpha 0.3.
Return list.

`cost_and_q(x)`:
```
q=np.convolve(x,x)
s=x.sum()
if s<=0: return inf,q
return 2*len(x)*q.max()/(s*s), q
```
In optimization, keep sum1; use `q.max()` normalized? If sum1, cost=2n qmax. But for arbitrary, use formula.

`active_gradient(x, q, threshold=0.9, max_active=0)`:
```
n=len(x); M=float(q.max())
if M<=0: return None
idx=np.where(q > M*threshold)[0]
if len(idx)==0: idx=np.argmax(q) mech
if max_active>0 and len(idx)>max_active:
   # keep top max_active
   top=np.argpartition(q, -max_active)[-max_active:]
   idx=top
G=np.zeros(n)
for k in idx:
   start=max(0,k-(n-1)); stop=min(n-1,k)+1
   G[start:stop] += x[start:stop][::-1]  # already includes 2? We'll multiply by 2 later
return 2.0*G
```
If all active, summing all active could be large. We might instead use weighted gradient from softmax of top constraints:
```
w = (q[idx]-M*threshold)/(M*(1-threshold+eps))
```
or uniform. We can expose `alpha`.

`subgradient_candidate(x, q, rng, step, threshold)`:
- compute G.
- scale = max(G)
- if scale small return x
- y = np.log(x+1e-300) - step * (G/scale)
- maybe add small noise to log: y += rng.normal(0, 1e-3, n)
- new=np.exp(np.clip(y, -30, 30)); normalize.
- If sum too small, fallback.
We can try a few step signs? Only negative G to reduce active variables. Could also use positive on low constraints? Maybe not.

`multiplicative_block(x, rng, sigma, wmin, wmax)`:
```
z=x.copy()
width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n*wmin), max(2,n*wmax)))
start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
z[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,size=width))
# optionally small elements to zero? ensure positive by adding floor
return normalize(np.clip(z,0,1000))
```
For active reduction, we can target high-contributing indices: choose random active variable and decrease.

`coordinate_step(x, q, rng)`: pick r indices weighted by gradient and perturb.

`spectral_candidate(x, alpha, nfft=None)`:
```
n=len(x); if nfft is None: nfft=1
while nfft<2*n+1: nfft*=2
F=np.fft.rfft(x,nfft)
T=np.ones(2*n-1)
T_hat=np.fft.rfft(T,nfft)
amp=np.sqrt(np.abs(T_hat)+1e-12)
absF=np.abs(F); absF[absF<1e-12]=1e-12
phase=F/absF
F2=phase*amp
y=np.fft.irfft(F2,nfft)[:n]
# y may negative; use positive part plus fallback x
yp=np.clip(y,0,1000)
s=yp.sum()
if s<=1e-12: return x.copy()
yc=yp/s
# blend in log domain
logx=np.log(x+1e-300); logy=np.log(yc+1e-300)
zb=np.exp((1-alpha)*logx + alpha*logy)
return normalize(zb)
```
But target T all ones over length 2n-1; for n large, T_hat has zeros; amp zero there, phase*zero fine. This could kill some frequencies. Maybe use target `T = np.ones(2*n-1)` with small ramp? Could also use target current q clipped: T = np.clip(q, 0, M) smoothed. Not.

LP step:
```
def lp_direction(x, gmax=10.0, prefer_speed=False):
 if not HAS_SCIPY: return None
 n=len(x)
 M=float(np.convolve(x,x).max())
 if M<=0: return None
 rows=[]; cols=[]; data=[]
 for k in range(2*n-1):
   start=max(0,k-(n-1)); stop=min(n-1,k)+1
   idx=np.arange(start,stop)
   vals=x[start:stop][::-1]
   rows.extend([k]*len(idx)); cols.extend(idx.tolist()); data.extend(vals.tolist())
 A=sp.csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)),shape=(2*n-1,n))
 c=-np.ones(n)
 b=np.full(2*n-1, M*(1+1e-9))
 bounds=[(0.0,gmax)]*n
 res=linprog(c,A_ub=A,b_ub=b,bounds=bounds,method='highs',options={'presolve':True,'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-8})
 if res.success:
   g=np.asarray(res.x,float); return normalize(np.clip(g,0,None)), gmax
 return None
```
Building data with `tolist` slow for n=512 (65k) okay. We can cache indices and build data more efficiently:
Precompute `lp_meta(n)`: rows, cols, and for each row start,stop? Top-level dictionary? Since no closures but can use dictionary global. Simpler in function build loop; okay few calls.
We can pass `gmax` maybe 10*max(x)+1. If normalized n=256, max≈0.1, gmax=2? But LP could need >2; use 100.0.
If objective res.fun <= -1e-6 (sum <1), fallback x.
Update: try t in [0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.75,1.0] and evaluate cost; choose best.

`optimize_sequence(x0, budget_s, seed, use_lp=True, max_lp=...)`:
We can implement with global best passed? Maybe a top-level function that takes and returns.
Pseudo:
```
def optimize_sequence(x0, budget_s, seed, do_lp=False, lp_max=3):
  n=len(x0); start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s
  rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
  x=normalize(x0.copy())
  best=x.copy(); bestq,_=... bestcost=cost
  lp_used=0
  no_improve=0
  step=0.05
  while time<deadline:
    # choose op
    r=rng.random()
    if do_lp and lp_used<lp_max and (r<0.1 or no_improve>50) and n<=512:
       cand=lp_candidate...
       if cand is not None:
          cand, c = cand
          # line search mix
          best_cand=x; best_c=bestcost
          for t in [0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0]:
             z=normalize((1-t)*x + t*cand); cost=eval_cost(z)
             if cost<best_c: best_c=z; best_c=cost
          if best_c<bestcost: x=best_c; best=...; no_improve=0; lp_used++; continue
    # subgradient with random active threshold/step
    q=convolve(x,x)
    th = 0.95 if no_improve<20 else 0.85
    cand=subgradient(x,q, step, th, rng)
    # also block perturb if r
    if rng.random()<0.3: cand=block_perturb(cand or x,...)
    cost=eval_cost(cand)
    if cost<bestcost:
       x=cand; best=cand; bestcost=cost; no_improve=0; step=max(0.01, step*0.95)
    else:
       no_improve+=1
       if no_improve>100:
          # reinitialize from best with larger block or random
          x=block_perturb(best, 1.0)
          step=0.2
  return best
```
But this restarts x from best? If no improve, x may have drifted bad. We should periodically set x=best.copy() and make larger perturbation.
Also evaluate best prefix at start.

We can improve by attempting multiple step sizes for subgradient without many evals: For a given G, try step values [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0] and pick best. But each step candidate cost; maybe 3. We can do if time.

```
def optimize_sequence(x0, budget, seed, use_lp=False, max_lp=2):
  n=x0.size; rng=np.random.default_rng(seed); t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget
  x=_normalize(x0.copy())
  best=x.copy(); best_cost=_cost(x)
  no=0; step=0.08; lp_used=0; last_lr=t0
  # maybe initial quick local: while time < t0+budget*0.6:
  while time.monotonic()<deadline:
     # If no improve many, reset x to best with perturbation
     if no>200:
        x=_block_perturb(best, rng, 0.8,0.05,0.5)
        no=0; step=0.15
     r=rng.random()
     if use_lp and n<=512 and lp_used<max_lp and (r<0.05 or no>100):
        g=_lp_candidate(x)
        if g is not None:
           cands=[]
           for t in (0.1,0.2,0.4,0.7,1.0):
              z=_normalize((1-t)*x + t*g)
              cands.append(z)
           # evaluate each
           for z in cands:
              c=_cost(z)
              if c<best_cost:
                 best=z.copy(); best_cost=c; x=z.copy(); no=0; break
           lp_used+=1
           if lp_used==max_lp and no>0: no=0
           continue
     q=np.convolve(x,x)
     thresh = 0.98 if no<50 else (0.92 if no<150 else 0.8)
     max_active = 0
     if r<0.2:
        cand=_subgrad_candidate(x,q,step,thresh,rng,max_active=20)
     elif r<0.5:
        cand=_subgrad_candidate(x,q,step,thresh,rng,max_active=0)
     elif r<0.75:
        cand=_block_perturb(x,rng,0.25,0.02,0.25)
        # maybe combine with subgrad? 
     elif r<0.85:
        cand=_spectral_candidate(x, 0.3)
     else:
        cand=_roll_candidate(x,rng)
     c=_cost(cand)
     if c<best_cost:
        x=cand; best=x.copy(); best_cost=c; no=0
        step=max(0.02, step*0.97)
     else:
        # if not too bad and r subgrad, keep walking? We can still set x=cand if c < best_cost*1.05 to explore? Risk.
        no+=1
        if no % 50 ==0:
           step = min(0.8, step*1.1)
        if no>50:
           # partial reset: mix current and best
           cand2=_normalize(0.5*x+0.5*best)
           c2=_cost(cand2)
           if c2<best_cost:
              x=cand2; best=x.copy(); best_cost=c2; no=0
           else:
              x=_block_perturb(best, rng, 0.5, 0.05,0.5)
  return best, best_cost
```

`_subgrad_best` line search:
```
def _subgrad_best(x, threshold, step, max_active, noise, rng):
    q=np.convolve(x,x)
    M=float(q.max())
    if M<=0: return x.copy(), 2*x.size*M
    idx=np.flatnonzero(q >= M*threshold)
    if idx.size==0: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(q))], dtype=int)
    if max_active>0 and idx.size>max_active:
        idx=np.argpartition(q, -max_active)[-max_active:]
    G=np.zeros(x.size)
    n=x.size
    for k in idx:
       k=int(k); start=k-n+1; if start<0: start=0; stop=k+1; if stop>n: stop=n
       if stop>start: G[start:stop] += x[start:stop][::-1]
    Gmax=float(G.max())
    if Gmax<=1e-300:
       return x.copy(), 2*n*M
    G=G/Gmax
    base=np.log(x+1e-300)
    bestx=x.copy(); bestc=float(2*n*M)
    steps=[step*0.25, step*0.5, step, step*2.0, step*4.0]
    for s in steps:
       if s<=0: continue
       y=base - s*G
       if noise>0:
          y=y + rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
       z=np.exp(np.clip(y, -60.0, 60.0))
       z=np.clip(z,0,1000)
       zsum=z.sum()
       if zsum>1e-300:
          z=z/zsum
          c=float(2*n*np.convolve(z,z).max())
          if c<bestc:
             bestx=z; bestc=c
    return bestx,bestc
```
Because we normalize x to sum1, cost=2n max q. Good. If x not sum1 due ensure, okay.

But if we call with x sum1, q max M. The candidate cost uses convolve each step; could reuse? no.

`_block_perturb` returns normalized; cost computed outside.

`_optimize_sequence` more refined:
```
def _optimize_sequence(x0, budget, seed, use_lp=False, max_lp=0):
  n=x0.size; tstart=time.monotonic(); deadline=tstart+budget
  x=_normalize(x0.copy())
  best=x.copy(); _, bestc = _cost1(x) # cost normalized
  rng=...
  step=0.1; no=0; lp_count=0; last_spectral=0
  while time.monotonic()<deadline:
     if budget small break
     # choose operation based on no
     if use_lp and n<=512 and lp_count<max_lp and (no>80 or (lp_count==0 and time.monotonic()>tstart+budget*0.2)):
         g=_lp_candidate(x)
         if g is not None:
             # line search
             improved=False
             for t in (0.1,0.2,0.35,0.6,0.85,1.0):
                 z=_normalize((1-t)*x + t*g)
                 _,zc=_cost1(z)
                 if zc<bestc:
                    x=z; best=z.copy(); bestc=zc; no=0; improved=True; break
             lp_count+=1
             if improved: continue
     # subgrad with adaptive threshold
     if no < 30: th=0.98
     elif no<80: th=0.95
     elif no<200: th=0.9
     else: th=0.75
     max_active=0
     if no>100 and rng.random()<0.3: max_active=min(n, 32)
     cand,candc=_subgrad_best(x, th, step, max_active, 0.0, rng)
     if candc<bestc:
        x=cand; best=x.copy(); bestc=candc; no=0
        step=max(0.02, step*0.98)
     else:
        no+=1
        if no==10:
           cand2=_block_perturb(best, rng, 0.25, 0.02,0.2)
           _,c2=_cost1(cand2)
           if c2<bestc: x=cand2; best=x.copy(); bestc=c2; no=0
        elif no==30:
           cand2=_spectral_candidate(x, 0.25)
           _,c2=_cost1(cand2)
           if c2<bestc: ...
        elif no==60:
           cand2=_block_perturb(best, rng, 0.6,0.05,0.5)
           ...
        elif no==120:
           x=_block_perturb(best, rng, 1.0,0.1,0.8)
           no=0; step=0.3
        if no % 25 ==0:
           step=min(1.0, step*1.15)
  return best,bestc
```

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(60, float(budget_s)-5)
    deadline=t0+budget
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_seq=None; best_score=np.inf
    # consider height_sequence_1 if available
    h=None
    try: h=np.asarray(height_sequence_1,float)
    except: h=None
    if h is not None and h.size>0:
       hn=_normalize(h)
       cs=_cost1(hn)
       if cs<best_score: best_seq=hn; best_score=cs
       print('height score', cs, len(hn))
    # quick prefixes
    prefix_scores=[]
    for n in [128,256,512,1024,2048]:
       if n==0 or n>4096: continue
       a=_prefix_unnorm(n); S=a.sum(); c=float(2*n/(S*S))
       prefix_scores.append((c,n))
       if c<best_score:
          best_seq=_normalize(a); best_score=c
       print('prefix', n, c)
    # choose lengths and allocate
    tasks=[]
    # main large subgrad
    remaining=max(0, deadline-time.monotonic())
    # allocate fractions
    if 400<remaining: tasks.append((2048, 0.4, False, 0))
    if 300<remaining: tasks.append((1024, 0.2, False,0))
    if 200<remaining: tasks.append((512, 0.2, True, 3))
    if 100<remaining: tasks.append((256, 0.1, True, 3))
    if 60<remaining: tasks.append((128, 0.1, True, 2))
    # avoid total > remaining: scale
    total_frac=sum(t[1] for t in tasks)
    for n,frac,use_lp,max_lp in tasks:
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline-3: break
       alloc=(deadline-time.monotonic())*0.9
       budget_task=alloc*(frac/total_frac)
       if budget_task<10: continue
       # choose init: if h len close? use prefix n
       init=_make_prefix(n)
       # If h exists and n? no
       seq, score=_optimize_sequence(init, budget_task, seed + n + (1000 if use_lp else 0), use_lp=use_lp, max_lp=max_lp)
       if score<best_score:
          best_seq=seq; best_score=score
       print('task n',n,'score',score)
       # If found under 1.4, break? Keep best.
       if best_score < 1.45:
          # still return, but could use remaining to polish
          pass
    # If best_seq None, use prefix 1024
    if best_seq is None:
       best_seq=_make_prefix(1024); best_score=_cost1(best_seq)
    # Ensure final length not too huge and sum scale 10
    arr=np.asarray(best_seq,float)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s < 10.0:
       factor=10.0/max(s,1e-12)
       arr=arr*factor
    # clean
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0)
    # final verify
    try:
       ev=evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist())
       if not np.isfinite(ev):
           # fallback prefix 1024
           arr=_make_prefix(1024); arr*=10.0
    except Exception:
       pass
    print('final score internal', best_score)
    return [float(v) for v in arr]
```

    if x.size==0: return np.inf

    m=float(q.max())

def _block_perturb(x, rng, sigma, wmin, wmax):
    n=x.size; z=x.copy()
    if n>1:
       lo=int(max(1, int(n*wmin)))
       hi=int(max(lo+1, int(n*wmax)))
       width=int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
       start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))

    # Add small floor to prevent all zeros
    z=np.clip(z,0,1000)
    # If many zeros, add tiny
    if z.sum() <= 0:
       z=np.ones(n)
    return _normalize(z)

Let's write final code in final block.

```python
"""
Search for low-score non-negative step coefficients.

The objective is
    2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

The main idea in this program is to initialize with the one-sided
Hankel/recurrence sequence whose square is flat on the first half:
    a_0 = 1,
    a_k = 1/2 * (1 - sum_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i}),
which asymptotically gives a score near pi/2.  From there we run a
monotone projected-subgradient / LP / spectral polishing search over
several sequence lengths, keeping the best sequence found.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    import scipy.sparse as _sp
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    _HAS_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _HAS_SCIPY = False


def _next_pow2(m):
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _normalize(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones_like(x, dtype=np.float64) / float(x.size)
    return x / s


def _cost_normalized(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    q = np.convolve(x, x)
    m = float(q.max())
    return float(2.0 * float(x.size) * m)


def _prefix_unnorm(n):
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, n):
        if k == 1:
            r = 0.0
        else:
            r = float(np.dot(a[1:k], a[k - 1:0:-1]))
        v = 0.5 * (1.0 - r)
        a[k] = v if v > 0.0 else 0.0
    return a


def _make_prefix(n):
    return _normalize(_prefix_unnorm(n))


def _block_perturb(x, rng, sigma, wmin, wmax):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    z = x.copy()
    if n > 1:
        lo = max(1, int(n * wmin))
        hi = max(lo + 1, int(n * wmax))
        width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        if width > 0:
            z[start:start + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=width)))
    z = np.clip(z, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if z.sum() <= 1e-300:
        z = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return _normalize(z)


def _roll_candidate(x, rng):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if n <= 1:
        return x.copy()
    shift = int(rng.integers(1, n))
    y = np.roll(x, shift)
    return _normalize(y)


def _subgrad_best(x, threshold, step, max_active, noise, rng):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    q = np.convolve(x, x)
    m = float(q.max())
    current_cost = float(2.0 * n * m)
    if m <= 0.0:
        return x.copy(), current_cost

    idx = np.flatnonzero(q >= m * threshold)
    if idx.size == 0:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(q))], dtype=int)
    if max_active > 0 and idx.size > max_active:
        idx = np.argpartition(q, -max_active)[-max_active:]

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in idx:
        k = int(k)
        start = k - n + 1
        if start < 0:
            start = 0
        stop = k + 1
        if stop > n:
            stop = n
        if stop > start:
            g[start:stop] += x[start:stop][::-1]

    gmax = float(g.max())
    if gmax <= 1e-300:
        return x.copy(), current_cost
    g = g / gmax

    base = np.log(x + 1e-300)
    best_x = x.copy()
    best_c = current_cost
    steps = [0.25 * step, 0.5 * step, step, 2.0 * step, 4.0 * step]
    for st in steps:
        if st <= 0.0:
            continue
        y = base - st * g
        if noise > 0.0:
            y = y + rng.normal(0.0, noise, size=n)
        z = np.exp(np.clip(y, -60.0, 60.0))
        z = np.clip(z, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(z.sum())
        if s > 1e-300:
            z = z / s
            c = _cost_normalized(z)
            if c < best_c:
                best_c = c
                best_x = z
    return best_x, best_c


def _spectral_candidate(x, alpha):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if n <= 1:
        return x.copy()
    nfft = _next_pow2(max(2 * n + 1, 256))
    f = np.fft.rfft(x, n=nfft)
    target = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    target_hat = np.fft.rfft(target, n=nfft)
    amp = np.sqrt(np.abs(target_hat) + 1e-12)
    absf = np.abs(f)
    absf[absf < 1e-12] = 1e-12
    phase = f / absf
    f2 = phase * amp
    y = np.fft.irfft(f2, n=nfft)[:n]
    yp = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(yp.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return x.copy()
    yc = yp / s

    logx = np.log(x + 1e-300)
    logy = np.log(yc + 1e-300)
    z = np.exp((1.0 - alpha) * logx + alpha * logy)
    z = np.clip(z, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if z.sum() <= 1e-300:
        return x.copy()
    return z / z.sum()


def _lp_candidate(x):
    if not _HAS_SCIPY:
        return None
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if n <= 1 or n > 512:
        return None
    m = float(np.convolve(x, x).max())
    if m <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(m):
        return None

    rows = []
    cols = []
    data = []
    for k in range(2 * n - 1):
        start = max(0, k - n + 1)
        stop = min(n - 1, k) + 1
        if stop <= start:
            continue
        idx = np.arange(start, stop)
        vals = x[start:stop][::-1]
        rows.extend([k] * len(idx))
        cols.extend(idx.tolist())
        data.extend(vals.tolist())

    try:
        aub = _sp.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n))
        c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.full(2 * n - 1, m * (1.0 + 1e-9), dtype=np.float64)
        gmax = max(10.0, 50.0 * float(x.max()))
        bounds = [(0.0, gmax) for _ in range(n)]

        try:
            res = linprog(
                c,
                A_ub=aub,
                b_ub=b,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True, "time_limit": 25.0},
            )
        except Exception:
            res = linprog(
                c,
                A_ub=aub,
                b_ub=b,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True},
            )

        if res is not None and res.success:
            g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if g.sum() > 1e-12:
                return _normalize(g)
    except Exception:
        return None
    return None


def _optimize_sequence(x0, budget, seed, use_lp, max_lp):
    x = _normalize(np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64).copy())
    best = x.copy()
    best_cost = _cost_normalized(best)
    n = x.size

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(5.0, float(budget) - 2.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    step = 0.08
    no_improve = 0
    lp_used = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # LP polishing, only for moderate lengths and only a few times.
        if use_lp and n <= 512 and lp_used < max_lp:
            if lp_used == 0 and (deadline - time.monotonic()) > 10.0:
                lp_used += 1
                g = _lp_candidate(x)
                if g is not None:
                    improved = False
                    for t in (0.1, 0.25, 0.45, 0.7, 1.0):
                        z = _normalize((1.0 - t) * x + t * g)
                        c = _cost_normalized(z)
                        if c < best_cost:
                            x = z
                            best = z.copy()
                            best_cost = c
                            no_improve = 0
                            improved = True
                            break
                    if improved:
                        continue
            elif no_improve > 160:
                lp_used += 1
                g = _lp_candidate(x)
                if g is not None:
                    for t in (0.2, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0):
                        z = _normalize((1.0 - t) * x + t * g)
                        c = _cost_normalized(z)
                        if c < best_cost:
                            x = z
                            best = z.copy()
                            best_cost = c
                            no_improve = 0
                            break
                    continue

        if no_improve < 25:
            threshold = 0.985
        elif no_improve < 70:
            threshold = 0.96
        elif no_improve < 160:
            threshold = 0.92
        else:
            threshold = 0.82

        max_active = 0
        if no_improve > 120 and rng.random() < 0.25:
            max_active = min(n, 48)

        noise = 0.0
        if no_improve > 90:
            noise = 0.01 + 0.02 * min(1.0, (no_improve - 90.0) / 200.0)

        cand, cand_cost = _subgrad_best(x, threshold, step, max_active, noise, rng)
        if cand_cost < best_cost:
            x = cand
            best = x.copy()
            best_cost = cand_cost
            no_improve = 0
            step = max(0.02, 0.98 * step)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve == 12:
                z = _block_perturb(best, rng, 0.25, 0.02, 0.25)
                c = _cost_normalized(z)
                if c < best_cost:
                    x = z
                    best = z.copy()
                    best_cost = c
                    no_improve = 0
            elif no_improve == 35:
                z = _spectral_candidate(x, 0.25)
                c = _cost_normalized(z)
                if c < best_cost:
                    x = z
                    best = z.copy()
                    best_cost = c
                    no_improve = 0
            elif no_improve == 70:
                z = _block_perturb(best, rng, 0.65, 0.05, 0.5)
                c = _cost_normalized(z)
                if c < best_cost:
                    x = z
                    best = z.copy()
                    best_cost = c
                    no_improve = 0
            elif no_improve == 140:
                x = _block_perturb(best, rng, 1.1, 0.1, 0.8)
                no_improve = 0
                step = min(1.0, step + 0.2)
            elif no_improve % 20 == 0:
                step = min(1.0, step * 1.12)

    return best, best_cost


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 8.0)
    deadline = t_start + budget
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    def consider(seq, score):
        nonlocal best_seq, best_score
        if seq is None or not np.isfinite(score):
            return
        seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
        score = float(score)
        if score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_seq = _normalize(seq.copy())

    # Use the provided starting construction if available.
    try:
        h = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if h.size > 0:
            hn = _normalize(h)
            ch = _cost_normalized(hn)
            consider(hn, ch)
            print(f"provided sequence: n={h.size}, score={ch:.9f}", flush=True)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Quick prefix benchmarks.
    prefix_lengths = [128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096]
    for n in prefix_lengths:
        if n <= 0 or n > 8192:
            continue
        a = _prefix_unnorm(n)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s > 0.0:
            c = float(2.0 * n / (s * s))
            consider(a, c)
            print(f"prefix n={n}: score={c:.9f}", flush=True)

    # Allocate polishing time across several lengths.  Small/moderate lengths
    # allow the LP direction; longer lengths exploit the smoother asymptotics.
    tasks = []
    # (length, fraction, use_lp, max_lp)
    tasks.append((2048, 0.30, False, 0))
    tasks.append((1024, 0.18, False, 0))
    tasks.append((4096, 0.14, False, 0))
    tasks.append((512, 0.18, True, 3))
    tasks.append((256, 0.10, True, 3))
    tasks.append((128, 0.10, True, 2))

    total_frac = sum(t[1] for t in tasks)
    for n, frac, use_lp, max_lp in tasks:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 4.0:
            break
        remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
        alloc = remaining * 0.92 * (frac / total_frac)
        if alloc < 8.0:
            continue

        init = _make_prefix(n)
        # Occasionally start from a perturbed prefix to avoid a stationary point.
        if rng.random() < 0.35:
            init = _block_perturb(init, rng, 0.15, 0.05, 0.35)

        seq, score = _optimize_sequence(init, alloc, int(seed + 1000 * n + (17 if use_lp else 0)), use_lp, max_lp)
        consider(seq, score)
        print(f"optimize n={n}, use_lp={use_lp}: score={score:.9f}, best={best_score:.9f}", flush=True)

        if best_score < 1.45 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            # If already very good, keep polishing the current length a bit more.
            extra = max(10.0, min(60.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.2))
            seq2, score2 = _optimize_sequence(best_seq, extra, int(seed + 777 * n), use_lp, max_lp)
            consider(seq2, score2)
            print(f"extra polish n={n}: score={score2:.9f}, best={best_score:.9f}", flush=True)

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _make_prefix(2048)
        best_score = _cost_normalized(best_seq)

    # Final validation / fallback and safe scaling.
    arr = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s < 10.0:
        arr = arr * (10.0 / max(s, 1e-12))

    try:
        final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()))
        if not math.isfinite(final_score):
            arr = _make_prefix(2048) * 10.0
            final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        arr = _make_prefix(2048) * 10.0
        try:
            final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            final_score = best_score

    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

    print(f"final n={arr.size}, internal best={best_score:.9f}, evaluator={final_score:.9f}", flush=True)
    return [float(v) for v in arr]
```
